=== page 9 ===
Council Business Items
2.1 Receive an Update on the Downtown Parking Program and Discuss Potential Changes
to Downtown Parking Restrictions
Assistant Public Works Director Schneider presented the staff report. Staff answered questions
from the Council regarding whether these recommendations are consistent with those in the
original Parking Study; plans for enforcement; if the department will go back to “chalking” tires; if
staff has considered downtown resident permit parking and how that would work; number of
spots that would be changing; how many merchant permits are issued; if the change of hours
from 9:00 a.m. to 5:00 p.m. would impact the unregulated spots; if the Economic Development
Committee (EDC) has received this presentation; if anything can be done locally in terms of
enforcement such as using interns or volunteers; the amount of Revenue generated from the
Creek Park Lot; the number of spots in the Creek Park Lot; if the Creek Park Lot is underutilized
or at capacity on a daily basis; the reason why this lot might be underutilized; location of the
signage directing drivers to the Creek Park Lot; if “good parking karma” exists in San Anselmo.
Mayor Kullaway opened the meeting to public comments.
Benedetto, representing the Chamber of Commerce, thanked staff for last week’s very
productive meeting. He supports the plan. Enforcement and education are key.
Frank asked the Council to listen to the owners of the hair salons who would like to see a three-
hour time limit in the Creek Park Lot. He agreed that enforcement is important along with
identifying problems ahead of time.
Mr. Pat Townsley, business owner, stated the Creek Park Lot is a “ghost town” until 5:00 p.m.
People do not want to use the technology required to pay for the parking.
Mr. Danny Krebs stated San Rafael Avenue and others have been adversely impacted. There
is a lot of congestion in these neighborhoods. He asked if ten to twenty spaces in the Creek
Park Lot could be designated as employee parking.
Ms. Anita Mitchell, business owner, thanked the Council for repealing the paid parking program
on San Anselmo Avenue. The missing piece of the puzzle is paid parking in the Creek Park Lot.
Parking in that lot should be free.
Emmie, manager of a downtown business, asked why San Anselmo Avenue from Ross to
Bolinas is not included in the recommendations. The time limits should be changed from two
hours to four hours. The unrestricted parking on Sir Francis Drake Boulevard should remain.
Mayor Kullaway closed the meeting to public comments.
Councilmember Schlesinger stated the suggestion to move to more merchant parking on Sir
Francis Drake Boulevard makes sense. Some of the spaces at the Creek Park Lot could be
designated for merchant parking while leaving the remainder as paid parking. Changing this lot
to a four-hour limit makes sense. He is in favor of changing the enforcement from 7:00 a.m. to
6:00 p.m. to 9:00 a.m. to 5:00 p.m. He is in favor of staff recommendations #1, #3, and #4.
Councilmember Burdo stated they need better enforcement or the situation will not improve.
He supported changing the hours in the Creek Parking Lot, changing the time limit from 2-hours
to 4-hours on Sir Francis Drake Boulevard from Bank to Ross, enforcement hours from 9:00
a.m. to 5:00 p.m. town-wide, and the creation of a downtown parking sticker for residents.
3
9

=== page 10 ===
Councilmember Burke stated she supported all the recommendations except R-4. She would
allow merchant parking in the Creek Parking Lot. She supports a downtown parking sticker for
residents who live downtown who do not have permanent parking. Enforcement is a problem
and San Anselmo does not get the value out of what they pay to the Central Marin Police
Authority (CMPA)
Mayor Kullaway stated a lot of the traffic on San Anselmo Avenue is an attempt to bypass the
Hub. Parking should be made obvious so people do not have to drive around looking for a
space. She supports opening up the Creek Park Lot for merchants.
It was the consensus of the Council to support Recommendation R-1, R-2, R-3, and R-4
(including allowing merchant parking).
Town Manager Donery asked the Council to give staff some time to come up with a process for
issuing downtown parking stickers for downtown residents. He will present this to the Council at
an upcoming meeting.
Public Hearing
3.1 Public Hearing to Adopt an Ordinance to Repeal and Replace Title 10 “Planning and
Zoning”, Chapter 4 “Housing Developments Under Government Code Section 65852.21”
to Comply with State Law; Rename Chapter 10-4 to “Urban Lot Splits and Two-Unit
Development in Single Family Zones”; Repeal Section 10-2.711 “Urban Lot Splits
Pursuant to Government Code Section 66411.7”; and Make Associated CEQA Exemption
Findings.
Assistant Town Attorney Longfellow presented the staff report. Staff answered questions from
the Council regarding whether this applies to just lot splits and not tear-downs of an existing
building; the change in the height restrictions.
Mayor Kullaway opened the Public Hearing.
There were no comments.
Mayor Kullaway closed the Public Hearing.
Councilmember Burke noted the Council does not have a choice- they are simply implementing
the law.
M/s, Burdo/Schlesinger, Motion to read the attached ordinance by title only, an Ordinance of the
Town Council of the Town of San Anselmo Repealing and Replacing Title 10 “Planning and
Zoning”, Chapter 4 “Housing Developments Under Government Code Section 65852.21” to
Comply with State Law, Renaming Chapter 10-4 to “Urban Lot Splits and Two-Unit
Development in Single Family Zones”, Repealing Section 10-2.711 “Urban Lot Splits Pursuant
to Government Code Section 66411.7”, and Making Associated CEQA Exemption Findings.
AYES: Burdo, Burke, Schlesinger, Mayor Kullaway
ABSENT: Walker
M/s, Burdo/Schlesinger, Motion for the first reading of the Ordinance and waive future readings
in their entirety.
AYES: Burdo, Burke, Schlesinger, Mayor Kullaway
ABSENT: Walker

STAFF REPORT
TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO Item 1.2
MEETING DATE: 10/28/25
AGENDA TITLE: Financial Report on Recreation Fund
FROM: Mattie O’Grady – Director of Community Services
RECOMMENDATION: That the Town Council review the Recreation Fund revenue and
expenditure report for the period ending September 30, 2025.
BACKGROUND
The Recreation Department offers a wide variety of fee-based programs throughout the
fiscal year including sports programs and leagues, youth and adult classes, and
childcare and camps. Additional revenue is generated through facility rentals and
administrative fees.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
As of September 30, 2025, 25% of the fiscal year has elapsed and the Recreation Fund
is listed at 37% of projected revenues and 26% of projected expenditures.
Attached are the department’s capital outlay budget and the Recreation Fund revenue
and expenditure report. For this fiscal year, the Recreation Department has allocated
funds to support special events and older adult programming that are not expected to
be cost-recoverable.
For future historical comparison purposes, staff has developed the following table:

STAFF REPORT
TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO Item #:1.3
MEETING DATE: October 28, 2025
AGENDA TITLE: Revenue and Expenditure Reports, September 2025
FROM: Jeff Zuba, Finance & Admin Services Director
RECOMMENDATION: That Town Council consider approving the monthly year-to-
date Revenue and Expenditure Reports and corresponding activity for the period ending
through September 30, 2025.
SUMMARY
Every reporting period end, the Town of San Anselmo presents to the Town Council its
operations as shown on the Revenue and Expenditure reports for discussion and
approval.
BACKGROUND
The Revenue and Expenditure reports are year-to-date summaries of revenues and
expenditures from the Town’s financial system (Tyler Incode’) for both the current as
well as the prior corresponding fiscal year. These reports include the General Fund’s
operations given that most of the Town-level operational activities are processed
through the General Fund either directly or indirectly. This approval is to be obtained
once a month within the fiscal year.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
The revenue and expenditure data through September 30, 2025, is a compilation of all
operating activity that is year-to-date for the current and prior fiscal year. These reports
are sourced directly from the Town’s financial system, of which a corresponding
analysis has been performed identifying the key trends the Town Council should take
note of.

=== page 20 ===
As shown above, the Town’s expenditures base overall increased by $0.438M, which is
driven by compensation, retirement, outside services – public safety, and debt service:
 Compensation (Salaries & Wages and Benefits) increased by $0.241M
compared to the prior fiscal year, primarily due to the 6.0% cost of living
adjustment effective July 1, 2025, step adjustments, one-time payment, and
health insurance premiums.
 Retirement increased by $0.120M compared to the prior period due to the
Town’s CalPERS unfunded liability payment, cost of living, step adjustments, and
fully staffed positions.
 Outside Services decreased by $0.080M compared to the previous period due to
lower allocated costs for Animal Control Services with the County and Library
Marinet Membership, and fewer plan check fees for the month of July.
 Outside Services – Public Safety increased by $0.208M compared to the
previous fiscal year due to payments for Public Safety JPA’s, Central Marin
Police Authority, and Ross Valley Fire Department.
 Debt Service increased by $0.043M compared to the previous fiscal year due to
the repayment of the 2021 Pension Obligation Bonds. The bond payments are
due in August and February.
 Internal Service Fund Charge decreased by $0.070M compared to the previous
period due to decreased payment to the Internal Service Funds for Insurance
and vehicle replacement, offset by increases for Building Maintenance and
Information Technology.
As far as the other expenditure streams, these changes either slightly changed or
remained flat year-over-year, resulting in an immaterial change. All other changes are
taken together overall and are immaterial for further discussion and are expected given
the Town’s operations.

STAFF REPORT
TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO Item #:1.4
MEETING DATE: October 28, 2025
AGENDA TITLE: Agreement with Marin IT for Management and
Technician Support Services
FROM: Jeff Zuba, Finance & Admin Services Director
RECOMMENDATION: That the Town Council authorize the Town Manager to sign
a three-year agreement with Marin IT for information technology management and
technician support services commencing November 1, 2025 and ending October 31,
2028.
BACKGROUND
The Town of San Anselmo has five departments and no full-time in-house information
technology (IT) staff or personnel trained specifically in network administration. The
Finance & Administrative Services Director currently oversees IT planning,
cybersecurity initiatives, vendor management, and project coordination.
For more than fifteen years, the Town has contracted with Marin IT for managed IT
support services, including help desk and desktop support, network and system
administration, application support, and IT project assistance. Previous agreements
were approved by the Town Council in June 2022 and July 2023.
Staff has evaluated the potential of hiring full-time IT personnel; however, doing so
would likely exceed the cost of the Marin IT contract while limiting capacity to a single
individual rather than a full professional support team. As a benchmark, the City of San
Rafael’s IT Help Desk Supervisor earns approximately $10,205 per month, which after
applying the Town’s standard benefit rate results in an estimated annual cost of about
$169,000, exceeding the cost of the proposed Marin IT agreement.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
The proposed agreement continues the Town’s long-standing relationship with Marin IT
and provides uninterrupted daily IT support, proactive cybersecurity monitoring, system
maintenance, and technology modernization services.
Item #: 1.4
28

=== page 29 ===
Marin IT works extensively with local government agencies, including the City of
Sausalito, Town of Ross, City of Mill Valley, Town of Corte Madera, City of Novato, City
of Sebastopol, and City of Petaluma, as well as first-responder agencies such as the
Central Marin Police Authority and Novato Fire Protection District. This experience
provides valuable familiarity with municipal systems, emergency response needs,
public-sector compliance standards, and cybersecurity protocols.
In addition to maintaining core systems and resolving technical issues, Marin IT has
played a critical role in advancing the Town’s cybersecurity posture. Recent
accomplishments include implementation of multi-factor authentication, migration to the
Town’s .gov domain, deployment of enhanced email and network security controls,
removal of unsupported software, establishment of Protective DNS and Microsoft
security monitoring, regular vulnerability scanning, delivery of staff cybersecurity
training, and creation of a 24/7 Security Operations Center for threat detection and
response.
The proposed agreement includes a Bay Area CPI review at the 18-month mark,
applicable only to the Marin IT support services, to determine whether an adjustment is
warranted for the final 18 months of the support services portion of the agreement.
FISCAL IMPACT
The Town’s technician support rates remained stable for several years, with $5,480 per
month paid from June 2018 through September 2022 and $5,720 per month from
October 2022 to present. The increase to $7,540 per month for technician services
reflects several years without rate adjustments, during which Marin IT absorbed rising
labor and insurance costs, similar to financial pressures faced by public agencies.
Of the new Marin IT proposal, $150,080 per year is dedicated specifically to IT support
services. The quote also includes $30,477.58 for the Town’s annual workstation
replacement plan, which will fluctuate based on lifecycle needs. Remaining costs are
attributable to Cloud Services and Managed Services, which the Town would incur
regardless of whether IT services were provided by Marin IT, another vendor, or an
internal IT employee, as these services are tied to required cybersecurity, software, and
infrastructure systems, not the vendor.
The agreement includes a Bay Area CPI review at the 18-month mark, which applies
only to the Marin IT management and support services portion of the agreement, and
would determine whether a price adjustment is warranted for the final 18 months of
those support services. Funding will be paid from the Information Technology Fund and
is included or planned within the FY 2025–26, FY 2026–27, FY 2027–28, and FY2028-
29 operating budgets. The Finance & Administrative Services Director will continue to
oversee this contract.
GENERAL PLAN CONSISTENCY
Not applicable

10. INSURANCE.
A. Scope of Coverage. During the term of this Agreement, CONSULTANT shall

($1,000,000) per occurrence, two million dollars ($2,000,000) general aggregate, and one million
dollars ($1,000,000) products and completed operations aggregate, for death, bodily injury, personal
injury, or property damage.
2. An automobile liability coverage (owned, non-owned, and hired vehicles)
using ISO Business Auto Coverage form CA 00 01 (or equivalent) in the minimum amount of one
million dollars ($1,000,000) dollars each accident combined single limit.
3. If any licensed professional performs any of the services required to be
performed under this Agreement, a professional liability insurance policy in the minimum amount of
one million dollars ($1,000,000) per claim, and two million dollars ($2,000,000) aggregate, to cover
any claims arising out of the CONSULTANT's performance of services under this Agreement.
Where CONSULTANT is a professional not required to have a professional license, TOWN
reserves the right to require CONSULTANT to provide professional liability insurance pursuant to
this section.
4. If it employs any person, CONSULTANT shall maintain worker's
compensation insurance, as required by the State of California, with statutory limits, and
employer’s liability insurance with limits of no less than one million dollars ($1,000,000) each
accident for bodily injury or disease. CONSULTANT’s worker’s compensation insurance shall
be specifically endorsed to waive any right of subrogation against TOWN.
5. CONSULTANT will at all times during the term of this Agreement
maintain Cyber Liability Insurance, with limits not less than $2,000,000 per occurrence or claim,
$2,000,000 aggregate. Coverage shall be sufficiently broad to respond to the duties and obligations
as is undertaken by CONSULTANT in this Agreement and shall include, but not be limited to,
claims involving security breach, system failure, data recovery, business interruption, cyber
extortion, social engineering, infringement of intellectual property, including but not limited to
infringement of copyright, trademark, trade dress, invasion of privacy violations, information theft,

Scope of Support Services
As part of this Proposal, it is our understanding that Marin IT will be responsible for support required for the customer to continue
daily operations, including but not limited to:
Data Systems:
 Remediation of support tickets opened by end users
 Real-time monitoring using remote management software on a specified set of devices. (Workstations, Servers, and
Network Devices). ***This service requires purchasing remote management / monitoring as part of this
agreement***
 End user device and peripheral maintenance, workstations, printers, scanners, keyboard, mouse, etc.
 Server maintenance - OS patching/hot fixes, server logs
 System administrator - Active directory (user accounts, permissions, and access to local resources), DHCP, DNS, MFA,
Etc
 Network device maintenance – OS patching / hot fixes (Switches, Firewalls, Wireless, Etc)
 Manage Microsoft Office 365 - User accounts / integration with active directory
 Review Anti-Virus/security logs, verify definitions are up to date.
 Remote VPN client support including integration with active directory / MFA as required
 Day to day assistance with installation of new equipment/applications (limited to individual user requests via support
ticket. Large projects must be quoted separately)
 Manage and maintain data backups
o Review logs and verify process is in working order.
o Perform corrective action when needed.
o Perform quarterly test restore or as requested by client.
Voip / Collaboration Systems:
 Assuming Marin IT is familiar with installed phone system provide support with day to day tasks for example; moves,
adds and changes (MAC).
 Coordinating support with phone system vendors and telephone provider as required.
 Assist with video conferencing systems / supporting users desktop conferencing - Teams, GoTo Meeting, Zoom, etc.
Scope of IT Mgmt Services
IT MANAGEMENT SUPPORT SERVICES
Essential Duties and Responsibilities
 Work with customer designee(s) to manage customers hardware, software, cloud-based and co-located services,
maintenance contracts and IT subscriptions.
 Manage network-based telecommunications, including telephone (VOIP) system, Video Conferencing, Office 365, etc.
 Work directly with customer designee(s) to identify IT priorities / design solution, prepare budget and work thru
implementation
 Assist with developing a Disaster Recovery environment to improve system recovery time the event of a catastrophic
equipment failure, or ransomware event. ***Any hardware and software required to build DR environment will
quoted and billed separately from this agreement***
 Recommend IT investments and/or upgrades that align with customers needs and goals.
 Prepare / make recommendations to improve technology process for efficiency and effectiveness.

SLA - Resposibilities
Marin IT’s Responsibilities:

IT will provide a contact number to reach our technicians after normal business hours. ***After hours / 24 hour support
requires purchasing after hours service as part of this agreement*** In the event that no 24 hour support is purchased with
this agreement after hours support will be provided with with a $250.00 call out fee and 2 hour minimum at overtime rates to be
approved by customer when support is requested.

Pricing/Rate Schedule / Invoicing:
This proposal is meant to provide support for the contracted number of hours but does not include overtime or special projects.
Any hours worked in addition to the contracted number of hours or additional projects will be chargeable at the rates described
below. This proposal does not include new hardware or software purchases / upgrades. Costs for hardware or software needed
to perform upgrades and/or replacements are not included and can be provided by Marin IT for additional costs.
Hours for support services outside of the regularly scheduled hours will be invoiced at Marin IT’s standard billing rates for our
technicians at the time the work is performed (currently $145 per hour). This rate, along with all other quoted rates is for work
during normal business hours (Monday through Friday between 8:30 AM and 5:00 PM), and special projects (not covered under
the scope of this proposal). Rates for overtime, nights, weekends, will be billed at 1 ½ times the standard rate. Holidays will be
billed at 2 times the standard rate.
Unless otherwise agreed to in writing by the parties, payment for Services by Marin IT rendered and any equipment, software,
licenses, supplied will be due within thirty (30) days from the date of the invoice for such services. If payment is not received
payment within thirty (30) days Marin IT shall have the option to discontinue services without any liability until payment is
received
Warranty:
Third Party Products purchased through Marin IT are warrantied by the products manufacturers and any applicable
manufacturer’s extended warranties / service agreements. All warranties will be passed to client. Marin IT will only accept
returns on Third Party Products if they are defective and returned within thirty (30) days of Client’s receipt of product
Force Majeure:
Neither party shall be deemed to have defaulted or breached hereunder, nor shall it hold the other party responsible for any
cessation, interruption or delay in the performance of its obligations hereunder due to earthquake, flood, fire, storm, natural
disaster, act of God, war, terrorism, hostile or warlike action including cyber or armed attacks in times of peace or war by a
government or sovereign power, labor strike, lockout, boycott, or other similar events beyond the reasonable control of such party
(collectively, “Force Majeure”), provided that the party relying upon this provision gives prompt written notice of any events, and
takes all steps reasonably necessary to mitigate the effects of the Force Majeure event.
Cancellation of Agreement:
This Agreement may be canceled at any time by either party at its discretion upon 60 days written notification. Marin IT is entitled
to receive full payment for all services performed and all costs incurred up to and including the date of receipt of written notice to
cease work. Marin IT shall be entitled to compensation for requested work performed after the date of receipt of written notice to
cease services. All products purchased for the customer shall become the property of the customer. In the event that either party
elects to terminate this Agreement Marin IT agrees to provide sufficient efforts and cooperation to ensure an orderly and efficient
transition of Services to Client or another service provider.

STAFF REPORT
TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO Item #:1.5
MEETING DATE: 10/28/25
AGENDA TITLE: Consideration of Special Event Application – 2025 Dia
de las Muertos
FROM: Jamie Bristow – Recreation Supervisor
RECOMMENDATION: That Council approve the Chamber of Commerce’s and
Taco Jane’s application for the Dia de las Muertos Aztec Dance performance scheduled
for Sunday, November 2, 2025, from 3:00 – 4:00 pm in Imagination Park.
BACKGROUND
Dia de Las Muertos (Day of the Dead) is a holiday celebrated on the 1st and 2nd of
November. It originated and is mostly observed in Mexico but also in other places,
especially by people of Mexican heritage elsewhere. The holiday involves family and
friends gathering to pay respects and to remember friends and family members who
have died.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
A small community group led by Taco Jane’s owner Matteo Boussina and the San
Anselmo Chamber of Commerce is interested providing a dance performance by a local
Aztec Dance group. The proposed location is Imagination Park and the proposed date
is Sunday, November 2, 2025 from 3:00 to 4:00 PM. There will be a procession
following the dance performance to Taco Jane's to continue the celebration in front of
the community alter. This group has held this celebration for the past several years in
Imagination Park without incident.
The attached application provides details of the events with comments and conditions
from Town staff.
FISCAL IMPACT
There is no fiscal impact to the Town related to this approval.
Item #: 1.5

CEQA AND CLIMATE ACTION PLAN CONSISTENCY
The proposed project is exempt from the California Environmental Quality Act (CEQA),
under the “common sense” exemption, because it can be seen with certainty that there
is no possibility that the activity in question may have a significant effect on the
environment. (Cal. Code Regs. tit. 14, § 15061).

STAFF REPORT
TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO Item #: 1.6
MEETING DATE: October 28, 2025
AGENDA TITLE: Second Reading and Adoption of Ordinance
Amendments to Repeal and Replace Title 10 “Planning
and Zoning”, Chapter 4 “Housing Developments Under
Government Code Section 65852.21” to Comply with
State Law; Rename Chapter 10-4 to “Urban Lot Splits
and Two-Unit Development in Single-Family Zones”;
Repeal Section 10-2.711 “Urban Lot Splits pursuant to
Government Code 66411.7”; and Make Associated
CEQA Exemption Findings.
FROM: Planning Department and Town Attorney’s Office
RECOMMENDATION: That the Town Council adopt the attached Zoning Ordinance
Amendments to repeal and replace Title 10 “Planning and Zoning”, Chapter 4 “Housing
Developments Under Government Code Section 65852.21” to comply with State law;
rename Chapter 10-4 to “Urban Lot Splits and Two-Unit Development in Single-Family
Zones”; repeal Section 10-2.711 “Urban Lot Splits pursuant to Government Code
66411.7”; and make associated CEQA exemption findings (see Attachment 1).
BACKGROUND
On October 14, 2025, the Town Council held a public hearing and approved the first
reading of the Ordinance. The amendments are required to comply with Senate Bill (SB)
450. A summary of the proposed amendments is included in the October 14, 2025, Town
Council staff report.
DISCUSSION
In 2021, SB 9 was enacted to address California’s housing crisis by allowing owners of
single-family properties to either develop up to two homes on a lot or split the lot in two
and build up to two homes per parcel. The Town Council adopted Ordinance No. 1159 in
2021 to implement the provisions of SB 9 locally.
Recently, the State adopted SB 450, which further streamlines implementation of SB 9
projects. The proposed Zoning Ordinance Amendments, displayed in Attachment 1,
would bring the Town’s Municipal Code into compliance with SB 450 by repealing
outdated SB 9 provisions, updating Chapter 10-4 to address both urban lot splits and two-
unit developments in single-family zones, and aligning local standards with State law
while preserving eligible objective standards, where allowed.
Item #: 1.6
69

=== page 70 ===
FISCAL IMPACT
There is no fiscal impact associated with the proposed Ordinance.
GENERAL PLAN CONSISTENCY
The proposed Ordinance is consistent with the General Plan because the General Plan
Housing Element encourages the development of new housing and the proposed
Amendments will facilitate housing production.
CEQA AND CLIMATE ACTION PLAN CONSISTENCY
The proposed Ordinance is intended to comply with SB 9 and SB 450 and are not
considered a “project” under the California Environmental Quality Act (“CEQA”), Division
13 (commencing with Section 2100) of the Public Resources Code, as provided in
Government Code sections 65852.21(j) and 66511.7(n). The Ordinance Amendments are
also exempt from CEQA per CEQA Guideline section 15301 involving the minor alteration
of existing residences involving negligible or no expansion of use beyond that already in
existence; Section 15303 New Construction or Conversion of Small Structures; and
pursuant to CEQA Guidelines section 15061(b)(3) because it can be seen with certainty
that there is no possibility they would have a significant effect on the environment; the
amendments make no or minimal changes to land uses, development intensities, or the
rate at which development is expected to occur.
The proposed Ordinance aligns with Climate Action Plan 2030 Measure T-9 (Smart
Growth Development), which calls for zoning regulations that allow for infill, higher
density, and mixed-use development near transit and within walking distance to shopping,
schools, and services.

ORDINANCE OF THE TOWN COUNCIL OF THE TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO
REPEALING AND REPLACING TITLE 10 “PLANNING AND ZONING”, CHAPTER 4
“HOUSING DEVELOPMENTS UNDER GOVERNMENT CODE SECTION 65852.21”
TO COMPLY WITH STATE LAW; RENAMING CHAPTER 10-4 TO “URBAN LOT
SPLITS AND TWO-UNIT DEVELOPMENT IN SINGLE-FAMILY ZONES”; REPEALING
SECTION 10-2.711 “URBAN LOT SPLITS PURSUANT TO GOVERNMENT CODE
66411.7”; AND MAKING ASSOCIATED CEQA EXEMPTION FINDINGS
WHEREAS, to increase the supply of housing, in 2021 California Senate Bill 9 (SB 9) was
passed adding Government Code section 65852.21, which requires local agencies to
ministerially approve an urban lot split to subdivide a residential lot into two separate lots
and construct a secondary home on the new lot, and an additional home on the original lot;
and
WHEREAS, among other things, SB 9 allowed jurisdictions to impose specific standards on
SB 9 projects such as: objective zoning and design standards that differed from the
underlying zone; a prohibition against demolishing more than 25% of a home’s existing
structural walls; and the requirement that the residential lot be split into two roughly equal
halves as small as 1,200 square feet; and
WHEREAS, California has adopted Senate Bill 450 (SB 450), effective January 1, 2025,
which amends Government Code section 65852.21 (SB 9), including in the following ways
for SB 9/450 projects:
 Only those objective standards applicable to the development within the
underlying zoning district apply;
 There is no limit that more than 25% of a home’s existing structural walls remain;
 A local agency must approve or deny an SB 9/450 project within 60 days from
the date of a completed application or it is deemed approved;
 Project cannot be denied based on specific, adverse impacts on the “physical
environment”; now only adverse impacts to public health and safety apply; and
WHEREAS, the ordinance amendments would repeal and replace Town Municipal Code,
Title 10, Chapter 4 “Housing Developments Under Government Code Section 65852.21”
to comply with State law set forth in SB 450, rename Town Municipal Code Chapter 10-4
to “Urban Lot Splits and Two-Unit Development in Single-Family Zones”, and repeal Town
Municipal Code Section 10-2.711 “Urban Lot Splits pursuant to Government Code
66411.7” (“Ordinance Amendments”); and
WHEREAS, the Planning Commission is responsible for providing recommendations to
the Town Council for proposed amendments to the Town’s Zoning Code pursuant to
Government Code section 65853 et seq.; and
WHEREAS, the Planning Commission held a duly noticed public hearing on September
15, 2025 and voted to recommend that the Town Council adopt the Ordinance
Amendments; and
WHEREAS, the Town Clerk caused notice of a Town Council public hearing regarding the
Town Council’s consideration of adoption of the Ordinance Amendments in the Marin
1
71

=== page 72 ===
Independent Journal newspaper on as required by Government Code section 36933 and
published the text of the Ordinance Amendments as required by State law; and
WHEREAS, on October 14, 2025, the Town Council held a duly noticed public hearing to
consider introduction of the Ordinance Amendments; and
WHEREAS, the Ordinance Amendments are exempt from the California Environmental
Quality Act (“CEQA”) pursuant to Public Resources Code section 21080.17, which
exempts the local adoption of ordinances regarding second units in single or multi-family
residential zones; CEQA Guidelines section 15301 involving the minor alteration of
existing residences involving negligible or no expansion of use beyond that already in
existence; and pursuant to CEQA Guidelines section 15061(b)(3) because it can be seen
with certainty that there is no possibility they would have a significant effect on the
environment; the amendments make minimal changes to land uses, development
intensities, and the rate at which development is expected to occur; and
WHEREAS, the proposed Ordinance Amendments are consistent with the General Plan
because the General Plan Housing Element encourages the development of new housing
and the proposed Amendments will facilitate housing production, and they are necessary
to comply with State law; and
WHEREAS, the adoption of the proposed Ordinance Amendments is in the public interest
because they encourage the development of increased housing and are necessary to
comply with State law; and
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT:
1. Based on substantial evidence in the record, the above recitals are true and correct
and incorporated as findings herein.
2. The Town Council of the Town of San Anselmo does hereby adopt the Ordinance
Amendments as set forth in Exhibit “A” and incorporated herein by reference.

Anselmo Town Council on October 14, 2025, and was adopted at a regular meeting of
the San Anselmo Council on October 28, 2025, by the following vote:

Exhibit A
SECTION 1: Ordinance Amendment. San Anselmo Municipal Code Section 10-2.711
“Urban Lot Splits pursuant to Government Code 66411.7” is hereby repealed in its
entirety.
SECTION 2: Ordinance Amendment. San Anselmo Municipal Code Title 10 “Planning
and Zoning”, Chapter 4 “Housing Developments Under Government Code section
65852.21” is hereby repealed in its entirety and replaced as follows:
Urban Lot Splits and Two-Unit Development in Single-Family Zones
Article 1. – Purpose and Applicability
10-4.101 – Purpose and Authority. This chapter provides the regulations required to
implement the provisions of California Government Code sections 65852.21 and
66411.7, as may be amended, and is necessary to protect public health, welfare, and
safety. In cases of conflict between this chapter and any other provisions of the Town
Municipal Code, this chapter shall prevail. To the extent any provision of this chapter
conflicts with State law, the applicable provision of State law shall control, but in all other
non-conflicting provisions, this chapter shall remain in full force and effect. The Town
Council enacts this ordinance under the authority granted to cities and towns by Article
XI, Section 7 of the California Constitution and Government Code sections 65852.21 et
seq. and 66411.7 et seq.
10-4.102 – Applicability. This chapter shall apply to all single-family residential zone
parcels in the Town of San Anselmo (the R-1, R-1-H, and R-1-C zoning districts).
Article 2. – Definitions
10-4.201 – Definitions. The following definitions apply to this chapter.
a. “Acting in concert with the owner” means a person that has common
ownership or control of the subject parcel with the owner of the adjacent
parcel, a person acting on behalf of, acting for the predominant benefit of,
acting on the instructions of, or actively cooperating with, the owner of the
parcel being subdivided.
b. "Adjacent parcel" means any parcel of land that is: 1) touching the parcel at
any point; 2) separated from the parcel at any point only by a public right-of-
way, private street or way, or public or private utility, service, or access
easement; or 3) separated from another parcel only by other real property
which is in common ownership or control of the applicant.
c. “ADU” or “Accessory Dwelling Unit” shall have the same meaning as set forth
in Municipal Code section 10-6.102.
d. “Attached ADU” or “Attached Accessory Dwelling Unit” shall have the same
meaning as set forth in Municipal Code section 10-6.102.
e. “Common ownership or control” means property owned or controlled by the
3
73

=== page 74 ===
same person(s) or entity, or by separate entities, in which any shareholder,
partner, member, or family member of an investor of the entity owns 10% (ten
percent) or more of the interest in the property.
f. g. h. i. j. “Detached ADU” or “Detached Accessory Dwelling Unit” shall have the same
meaning as set forth in Municipal Code section 10-6.102.
“Dwelling unit” includes an ADU (attached or detached), a JADU, and two-unit
development, and/or a primary dwelling unit.
"Low income household" has the meaning set forth in Health and Safety Code
section 50079.5.
“Moderate income household” has the meaning set forth in Health and Safety
Code section 50093.
“Primary dwelling unit” means the single-family residence on the property and
is the larger of the two if there is an existing ADU on the property.
k. “Sufficient for separate conveyance” means that each attached or adjacent
dwelling unit is constructed in a manner adequate to allow for the separate
sale of each unit in a common interest development as defined in Civil Code
section 1351, including residential a residential condominium, planned
development, stock cooperative, or community apartment project, or into any
other ownership type in which the dwelling units may be sold individually.
l. “Two-unit development” means a development that proposes no more than
two (2) new dwelling units, or proposes to add one (1) new dwelling unit to
one (1) existing dwelling unit, pursuant to the provisions of this chapter and
Government Code sections 65852.21, as may be amended.
m. “Urban lot split” means a subdivision of an existing parcel into no more than
two (2) separate parcels pursuant to the provisions of this chapter and
Government Code section 66411.7, as may be amended.
n. “Very low income household” has the meaning set forth in Health and Safety
Code section 50105.
Article 3. – Urban Lot Splits
10-4.301 – Application and Review. The Community Development Director or
designee shall ministerially review an application for an urban lot split with no
discretionary review or public hearing, and shall approve the application if the criteria in
this chapter and Government Code section 66411.7, as may be amended, are satisfied.
The application shall contain such information as reasonably requested by the Town
Planning Department and accompanied by the appropriate fee as established by Town
Council Resolution.
10-4.302 – Review Deadline and Requirements. An application for an urban lot split
shall be considered and approved or denied within sixty (60) days from the date the Town
receives a completed application. If the Town has not approved or denied the completed
application within sixty (60) days, the application shall be deemed approved. If the Town
denies an application for an urban lot split pursuant to this subparagraph, the Town shall,
within the sixty (60) day deadline, return in writing a full set of comments to the applicant
with a list of items that are defective or deficient and a description of how the application
4
74

=== page 75 ===
can be remedied by the applicant.
10-4.303 – Adverse Health and Safety Impacts. Notwithstanding anything to the
contrary in this chapter, the Community Development Director or designee may deny an
application for an urban lot split if the Town’s Building Official makes written findings,
based upon a preponderance of the evidence, that the proposed housing development
project would have a specific, adverse impact, as defined and determined in paragraph
(2) of subdivision (d) of Section 65589.5, upon public health and safety for which there is
no feasible method to satisfactorily mitigate or avoid the specific, adverse impact.
10-4.304 – Urban Lot Split Requirements. An application for an urban lot split pursuant
to this chapter shall meet the following requirements:
a. The parcel map divides an existing parcel to create no more than two (2)
new parcels of approximately equal lot area provided that one parcel shall
not be smaller than 40% (forty) of the lot area of the original parcel proposed
for subdivision.
b. c. d. Neither the newly created lot nor the original lot shall be smaller than 1,200
square feet.
Only residential uses are allowed on a parcel created by an urban lot split.
No more than two (2) dwelling units may be located on any lot created
through an urban lot split, including primary dwelling units, ADUs, JADUs,
density bonus units, and units created as a two-unit development.
e. The parcel must meet the following requirements:
a. The parcel is located in an R-1, R-1-H, or R-1-C zoning district.
2. The parcel is located within the Town, the boundaries of which include
some portion of either an urbanized area or urban cluster, as designated
by the United States Census Bureau, or, for unincorporated areas, a
legal parcel wholly within the boundaries of an urbanized area or urban
cluster, as designated by the United States Census Bureau.
3. The parcel satisfies the requirements specified in Government Code
section 65913.4(a)(6)(B) through (K), inclusive of paragraph (6) of
subdivision (a) of Section 65913.4, as that section read on September
16, 2021.
10-4.305 – Objective Standards for Urban Lot Split.
a. The urban lot split must conform to all applicable objective requirements of
the Subdivision Map Act (Division 2, commencing with Section 66410),
except as otherwise expressly provided herein.
b. Unless otherwise provided for in this chapter, the parcel shall conform with
the objective zoning standards and subdivision standards that apply
uniformly to the underlying zoning district.
c. d. Each newly created parcel shall have access to, provide access to, or adjoin
a right-of-way.
Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this section, no setback shall be
required for an existing dwelling unit or a dwelling unit constructed in the
same location and to the same dimensions as an existing dwelling unit.
5
75

=== page 76 ===
e. A setback of four (4) feet from the side and rear lot lines is required for a
newly created dwelling unit.
f. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this section, the Town shall not
impose objective zoning standards, objective subdivision standards, or
objective design review standards that would have the effect of physically
precluding the construction of two (2) dwelling units on either of the newly
created parcels or that would result in a dwelling unit size of less than 800
square feet.
10-4.306 – Conditions for Urban Lot Splits. The following applies regarding conditions
for an urban lot split:
a. The applicant shall execute easements, or show evidence of such
easements, necessary for the provision of public services and facilities.
b. Offstreet parking of up to one space per unit is required, except that no
parking shall be required in either of the following instances:
1. The parcel is located within one-half (1/2) mile walking distance of either
a high-quality transit corridor as defined in subdivision (b) of Section
21155 of the Public Resources Code or a major transit stop as defined
in Section 21064.3 of the Public Resources Code.
There is a car share vehicle located within one block of the parcel.
2. c. The Town shall not require dedications of rights-of-way or the construction
of offsite improvements for the parcel being created as a condition of issuing
a parcel map for an urban lot split.
d. The Town shall not require as a condition of approval for an urban lot split
the correction of nonconforming zoning conditions.
10-4.307 – Owner Occupancy.
a. The Town shall require an applicant for an urban lot split to sign an affidavit
stating that the applicant intends to occupy one of the dwelling units as the
principal residence for a minimum of three (3) years from the date of the
approval of the urban lot split.
b. This subdivision shall not apply to an applicant that is a “community land
trust,” as defined in clause (ii) of subparagraph (C) of paragraph (11) of
subdivision (a) of Section 402.1 of the Revenue and Taxation Code, or is a
“qualified nonprofit corporation” as described in Section 214.15 of the
Revenue and Taxation Code.
10-4.308 – No Short Term Rentals. Lots created by an urban lot split shall not be used
for rental units for a period of less than thirty (30) consecutive days.
10-4.309 – Not Eligible For Urban Lot Split. The following properties are not eligible
for an urban lot split under this chapter:
a. Any parcel that was created through prior exercise of an urban lot split
pursuant to this chapter and/or Government Code section 66411.7, as may
be amended.
b. Any parcel where the owner of the parcel being subdivided or any other
6
76

c. d. person or entity acting in concert with the owner has previously subdivided
an adjacent parcel using an urban lot split pursuant to this chapter
consistent with Government Code section 66411.7, as may be amended.
The proposed urban lot split would require demolition or alteration of any of
the following types of housing:
1. Housing that is subject to a recorded covenant, ordinance, or law
that restricts rents to levels affordable to persons and families of
moderate, low, or very low income.
2. Housing that is subject to any form of rent or price control through
a public entity's valid exercise of its police power.
3. Housing that has been occupied by a tenant in the last three (3)

The parcel is located within the following designated areas:
1. A historic district or property included on the State Historic

10. Lands under conservation easement.
Article 4. – Two-Unit Developments
10-4.401 – Application and Review. The Community Development Director or
designee shall ministerially review an application for a two-unit development with no
discretionary review or public hearing, and shall approve the application if the criteria in
this chapter and Government Code section 65852.21, as may be amended, are
satisfied. The application shall contain such information as reasonably requested by the
Town Planning Department and accompanied by the appropriate fee.
10-4.402 – Review Deadline and Requirements. An application for a two-unit
development shall be considered and approved or denied within sixty (60) days from the
date the Town receives a completed application. If the Town has not approved or denied
the completed application within sixty (60) days, the application shall be deemed
approved. If the Town denies an application for a two-unit development pursuant to this
subparagraph, the Town shall, within the sixty (60) day deadline, return in writing a full
set of comments to the applicant with a list of items that are defective or deficient and a
description of how the application can be remedied by the applicant.
10-4.403 – Adverse Health and Safety Impacts. Notwithstanding anything to the
contrary in this chapter, the Community Development Director or designee may deny an
application for a two-unit development if the Town building official makes a written finding,
based upon a preponderance of the evidence, that the proposed two-unit development
project would have a specific, adverse impact, as defined and determined in paragraph
(2) of subdivision (d) of Section 65589.5, upon public health and safety for which there is
no feasible method to satisfactorily mitigate or avoid the specific, adverse impact.
10-4.404 – Two Unit Development Requirements. An application for a two-unit
development pursuant to this chapter shall meet the following requirements:
a. The parcel subject to the proposed two-unit development is located in an
R-1, R-1-H, or R-1-C zoning district.
b. The parcel subject to the proposed two-unit development is located within
the Town, the boundaries of which include some portion of either an
urbanized area or urban cluster, as designated by the United States
Census Bureau, or, for unincorporated areas, a legal parcel wholly within
the boundaries of an urbanized area or urban cluster, as designated by
the United States Census Bureau.
c. The parcel subject to the proposed two-unit development satisfies the
requirements specified in Government Code section 65913.4(a)(6)(B)
through (K), inclusive of paragraph (6) of subdivision (a) of Section 65913.4,
as that section read on September 16, 2021.
d. An application shall not be rejected solely because it proposes adjacent or
connected structures, provided that the structures meet building code safety
standards and are sufficient for separate conveyance.
9
79

=== page 80 ===
10-4.405 – Objective Standards for Two Unit Developments.
a. Unless otherwise provided for in this chapter, a two-unit development shall
comply with all objective zoning, subdivision, and design standards that
apply uniformly to the underlying zoning district.
b. c. d. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this section, no setback shall be
required for an existing dwelling unit or a dwelling unit constructed in the
same location and to the same dimensions as an existing dwelling unit.
A setback of four (4) feet from the side and rear lot lines is required for a
newly created dwelling unit.
Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this section, the Town shall not
impose objective zoning standards, objective subdivision standards, or
objective design review standards that would have the effect of physically
precluding the construction of two (2) dwelling units under this chapter on
either of the newly created lots, or that would result in a dwelling unit size
of less than 800 square feet.
e. A two-unit development shall comply with all applicable building, fire, safety,
and environmental regulations including Title 9 Building Regulations, Title
5, Chapter 8, Urban Runoff Pollution Prevention and Title 7, Chapter 12,
Watercourses unless otherwise provided for in this chapter or as required
by State law.
f. The applicant shall acquire any required permits from the U.S. Army Corps
of Engineers (USACE), Regional Water Quality Control Board (RWQCB),
and California Department of Fish and Wildlife (CDFW) prior to issuance
of a building permit and prior to conducting construction activity (including
grading, vegetation removal, and site preparation) within any and all
Waters of the State or Water of the U.S. under the jurisdictions of USACE,
RWQCB or CDFW including: (1) a RWQCB Waste Discharge
Requirements permit and/or Section 401 Water Quality Certification; (2) a
CDFW Streambed Alteration Agreement pursuant to Section 1600 et seq.
of the California Fish and Game Code for impacts within the areas of
CDFW jurisdiction; and (3) a USACE permit pursuant to Section 404 of the
Clean Water Act.
10-4-406 – Conditions for Two-Unit Developments. The following applies regarding
conditions for a two-unit development.
a. Off-street parking of up to one space per unit is required, except that no
parking shall be required in either of the following instances:
1. The parcel is located within one-half (1/2) mile walking distance of
either a high-quality transit corridor as defined in subdivision (b) of
Section 21155 of the Public Resources Code or a major transit stop
as defined in Section 21064.3 of the Public Resources Code.
2. There is a car share vehicle located within one block of the parcel.
b. For dwelling units connected to an onsite wastewater treatment system, a
percolation test must have been completed within the last five (5) years, or,
if the percolation test has been recertified, within the last ten (10) years.
c. Prior to issuance of a building permit under this chapter, the applicant shall
10
80

=== page 81 ===
record a restrictive covenant and agreement in a form prescribed by the
Town Attorney that shall run with the land and provide that the property is
restricted to residential uses only, and any dwelling unit on the property may
be rented or leased only for a period of longer than thirty (30) days. The
Town Manager or designee is authorized to enter into the covenant and
agreement on behalf of the Town and to deliver any approvals or consents
required by the covenant.
10-4-407 – No Short Term Rentals. A two-unit development shall not be used for
rental units for a period of less than thirty (30) consecutive days.
10-4.408 – Not Eligible for Two-Unit Development. The following properties are not
eligible for a two-unit development pursuant to this chapter:
a. The proposed two-unit development would require demolition or alteration
of any of the following types of housing:
1. Housing that is subject to a recorded covenant, ordinance, or law
that restricts rents to levels affordable to persons and families of
moderate, low, or very low income.
2. Housing that is subject to any form of rent or price control through
a public entity's valid exercise of its police power.
3. Housing that has been occupied by a tenant in the last three (3)
years.
b. Any parcel subject to the proposed housing development where the owner
of the parcel has exercised the owner’s rights under Chapter 12.75
(commencing with Section 7060) of Division 7 of Title 1 to withdraw
accommodations from rent or lease within fifteen (15) years before the date
that the development proponent submits an application.
c. The proposed two-unit development is in a historic district or property
included on the State Historic Resources Inventory, as defined in Section
5020.1 of the Public Resources Code, or within a site that is designated
or listed as a Town landmark or historic property or district pursuant to a
Town ordinance.
Article 5. – Fees, Violations and Enforcement.
10-4.501 – Fees. The Town Council may establish and set by resolution all fees and
charges, and related provisions, as may be necessary to effectuate the purpose of this
chapter.
10-4.502 – Violations and Enforcement. The Town Attorney shall be authorized to
abate violations of this chapter and to enforce the provisions of this chapter and all
implementing agreements and affidavits by civil action, injunctive relief, and any other
proceeding or method permitted by law. Violations of this chapter shall also constitute a
public nuisance subject to abatement pursuant to the Municipal Code, and/or any other
remedy allowed in the Municipal Code or State law. Remedies provided for in this chapter
shall not preclude the Town from any other remedy or relief to which it otherwise would

be entitled under law or equity. All remedies are cumulative.

MEETING DATE: October 28, 2025
AGENDA TITLE: Appoint November 10 as the Next Regular Meeting of
the San Anselmo Town Council
FROM: Serge Avila, Town Clerk
RECOMMENDATION: That the Town Council appoint Monday November 10, 2025
at 7pm in Town Hall as the next regular meeting of the Town Council.
BACKGROUND
The Brown Act requires the Town Council to establish the dates for regular meetings by
ordinance (Cal. Gov. Code § 54954) and San Anselmo’s Municipal Code contains the
following provision (emphasis added):
SAMC 2-1.01 - Meetings.
The regular monthly meetings of the Council shall be held on the second
Tuesday and the fourth Tuesday of every month at 7:00 p.m. at the Town Hall.
The Council, at any regular meeting, may appoint another day, hour, or
place for the next ensuing regular monthly meeting or for any
other regular meeting. When the second Tuesday, the fourth Tuesday, or the
day appointed for the regular meeting is a legal holiday, the meeting appointed
for said day shall be held on the first business day thereafter at the same hour
and place as the Council may have appointed therefor at its last regular meeting.
Accordingly, to hold a regular meeting of the Town Council on a date other than the
second and fourth Tuesday, the Council appoints the alternative date at a preceding
regular meeting.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
Due to the Veteran’s Day holiday on Tuesday November 11, the Town Council hereby
appoints Monday November 10 at 7pm in Town Hall as the next regular meeting of the
Town Council.
FISCAL IMPACT
There is no fiscal impact associated with this action.

STAFF REPORT
TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO Item #: 1.8
MEETING DATE: October 28, 2025
AGENDA TITLE: Policy to Require the Display of Town-issued Permits by
the Development Services Department
FROM: Heidi Scoble, Community Development Director
RECOMMENDATION: That the Town Council adopt the attached Resolution requiring that
permits issued by the Town of San Anselmo’s Development Services Department be displayed
in a visible location on the job site for the duration of construction or permitted activity.
BACKGROUND
The Town of San Anselmo’s Development Services Department, consisting of the Community
Development Department and the Department of Public Works, currently issues a variety of
permits, including:
 Title 4: Permits related to private and street trees.
 Title 7: Permits for work within the public right-of-way, including encroachments, news
racks, and sidewalks, as well as flood protection, watercourse work, wells, and
parklets.
 Title 9: Building permits and related approvals.
Although permit issuance procedures are standardized, there is currently no requirement for
approved permits to be posted visibly on-site.
Posting permits on-site is widely recognized as a best practice among Marin jurisdictions.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
Requiring the display of permits in a visible location serves several important purposes. This
policy enhances transparency, supports compliance, and improves inspection efficiency across
all development activities in Town.
Key benefits include:
 Verification and compliance: Enables inspectors and staff to easily confirm that work
being conducted is authorized and consistent with approved plans.
 Transparency: Provides assurance to neighbors and the public that the work has been
reviewed and approved by the Town.
 Efficiency: Reduces field verification time for inspectors, helping minimize project delays
and administrative workload.
Item #: 1.8
84

=== page 85 ===
Best Practices from Other Jurisdictions:
Many nearby agencies already require permits to be visibly posted at job sites, including:
 City of San Rafael: Requires building permits to be “posted on-site in a location visible from
the public right-of-way.”
 City of Mill Valley: Mandates that all active building permits be “displayed at the job site until
final inspection approval.”
 County of Marin: Requires permit cards to be “posted on the front of the property or on a
fence where visible from the street.”
The proposed requirement for San Anselmo aligns with these regional standards and promotes
consistent expectations for contractors, property owners, and Town staff.
Proposed Implementation:
To ensure clarity and uniform compliance:
 All permits issued by the Development Services Department shall be displayed in a visible
location near the main entrance or street frontage of the property.
 Permits must remain posted throughout the duration of work until final inspection approval
or project closure.
 The Development Services Department will provide written instructions with each permit and
ensure applicants acknowledge this requirement at the time of issuance.
FISCAL IMPACT
There is no direct fiscal impact associated with this policy. Minor administrative updates to
permit issuance procedures can be accommodated within existing staff resources.
GENERAL PLAN CONSISTENCY
This action is consistent with the Town’s General Plan goals related to public transparency,
efficient service delivery, and promoting compliance with Town regulations.
CEQA AND CLIMATE ACTION PLAN CONSISTENCY
This action is exempt from the California Environmental Quality Act (CEQA) under Section
15061(b)(3) of the CEQA Guidelines, as it can be seen with certainty that the adoption of this
administrative policy will not have a significant effect on the environment.
This policy also supports the Town’s Climate Action Plan goals by improving staff efficiency and
reducing redundant site visits.
ATTACHMENT
Attachment 1- Proposed Resolution
Item #: 1.8
85

=== page 86 ===
RESOLUTION NO. XXXX
RESOLUTION OF THE SAN ANSELMO TOWN COUNCIL REQUIRING THE
DISPLAY OF TOWN-ISSUED PERMITS BY THE DEVELOPMENT SERVICES
DEPARTMENT IN A VISIBLE LOCATION
WHEREAS, the Town of San Anselmo issues building, encroachment, grading, tree removal, and
other development-related permits to ensure compliance with applicable codes and regulations; and
WHEREAS, the Town seeks to improve transparency, facilitate efficient inspections, and promote
compliance by requiring that issued permits be visibly displayed on the job site; and
WHEREAS, requiring the display of permits in a visible location is a common best practice among
neighboring jurisdictions, including the Cities of San Rafael, Mill Valley, and Santa Rosa, and the
County of Marin; and
WHEREAS, the Town Council finds that requiring visible display of permits will enhance public
awareness, streamline field inspections, and ensure that work being performed is properly
authorized;
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the Town Council of the Town of San Anselmo
that:
1. All Town-issued Development Services Department permits, including building, grading,
tree removal, and encroachment permits, shall be displayed in a visible location on the job
site, such as near the main entrance or street frontage, for the duration of permitted work.
2. The displayed permit shall remain posted until the work authorized under the permit has
been completed and approved by the Town.
3. The Development Services Department shall include this requirement as a standard
condition of approval and provide written guidance to permit holders at the time of
issuance.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that this Resolution shall take effect immediately upon adoption.

SUMMARY
The Treasurer’s Investment Report is a quarterly itemization of the market value of
Town investments. The purpose of this report is to present financial information for
review and discussion.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
The attached report itemizes the Town’s investments and states the market value of
each investment as of September 30, 2025. The Wells Fargo bank account is the
Town’s primary checking account used for the deposit and expenditure of operating
funds. The Bank of America checking account is a controlled account used for payroll
processing only.
The Town holds three investment accounts: one with Moreton Capital with funds held at
U.S. Bank, one with Wells Fargo Securities with funds held at Wells Fargo, and one with
RBC Capital Markets held at RBC. The investments held are allowable by the Town’s
investment policy.
The Town maintains two Local Agency Investment Fund accounts (LAIF) for the
purpose of maximizing interest income on funds not needed for immediate expenditure.
One LAIF account is for the investment of general operating funds. The other is for the
remaining series 2003 Measure G bond proceeds, required by law to be maintained in a
segregated account.

TOWN OF SAN ANSELMO Item #: 2.1

AGENDA TITLE: Introduce, read by title only and waive reading of an
ordinance to repeal and replace Title 3, Chapter 3,
Article 8 of the San Anselmo Municipal Code to Adopt
by Reference the 2025 Edition of the California Fire
Code, portions of the 2024 International Fire Code
with Certain Local Amendments and Additions
FROM: Rob Bastianon, Senior Fire Inspector
Ross Valley Fire Department
RECOMMENDATION
1.
2.
That the Town Council of San Anselmo holds a first reading of the proposed
ordinance.
That the Mayor read the title of the attached ordinance to amend, Title 3, Chapter
3, Article 8 of the San Anselmo Municipal Code to adopt by reference the 2025
Edition of the California Fire Code, portions of the 2024 International Fire Code
with certain local amendments and additions.
3.
That the Town Council makes a motion to waive further reading and hold first
reading of the ordinance.
4.
That the Town Council schedules a public hearing, second reading, and adoption
of the ordinance at the November 10, 2025 Town Council meeting.
5. That the Town Council deem the Public Hearing Notice sufficient for publication.
PROCEDURAL NOTE
Typically, the Town Council conducts the public hearing on a proposed ordinance at the
time of introduction and first reading. Government Code Sections 50022.1 et seq .
requires a slightly different procedure for an ordinance that is adopting another code by
reference, as is the case here. When a code is adopted by reference, the public
hearing must be scheduled after the first reading, i.e., at the time of the second reading
and adoption.
Item #: 2.1
91

=== page 92 ===
BACKGROUND
This item involves consideration of an ordinance, which would amend the San Anselmo
Municipal Code to adopt the 2025 Edition of the California Fire Code and 2024
International Fire Code with certain local amendments and additions. This adoption
process is required by the Town Council to stay current with the minimum requirements
of the State of California building and fire standards as determined by the California
Building Standards Commission.
Every three years the California Building Standards Commissions publishes model
codes such as the California Fire Code, Building Code, Mechanical Code, Plumbing
Code, etc. Once published, local agencies have 180 days to make additions or
amendments based on local conditions. If no changes are made during this 180-day
window, the model codes become effective. Based on the 180-day cycle, we have until
January 1, 2026 to make local changes.
For the last eight code adoption cycles, the Marin County Fire Prevention Officers have
met and cooperatively reviewed the model codes. This proposed ordinance represents
a cooperative effort to develop standard ordinance language. While some minor
changes occur between jurisdictions, the end result is a more consistent and
cooperative approach to fire safety issues, making it easier for contractors and
developers to work with each jurisdiction. The local amendments included in the
ordinance do not represent any significant changes from the current Code.
DISCUSSION/ANALYSIS
Other than the new code references, there are no significant changes in the proposed
Ordinance language as compared to the last Ordinance 1169 adopted by the Town
Council in 2022. The Fire Code is arranged and organized to follow sequential steps
that generally occur during plan review or inspection. The 2024 International Fire Code
(IFC), which California adopts with amendments as the 2025 California Fire Code, has
again been organized into 7 parts. Each part represents a broad subject matter and
includes the chapters that logically fit under the subject matter of each part. The 2024
IFC was organized to allow for future chapters to be conveniently and logically
expanded without requiring a major renumbering. Therefore this code adoption, as in
past adoptions, results in some renumbering.
Proposed changes include:
●
Adoption of the 2025 Edition of the California Fire Code and 2024
International Fire Code with certain local amendments and additions.
● Amend Chapter 5, Section 503 requiring 15 feet of vertical clearance over fire
lanes.
Item #: 2.1
92

=== page 93 ===
● Amend Chapter 5, Section 510 by deleting Exception #4. Emergency
responder radio coverage in single story buildings less than 12,000 square
feet.
● Adopt Appendix D - Fire Apparatus Access Roads
FISCAL IMPACT
None
CEQA AND CLIMATE ACTION PLAN CONSISTENCY
In addition, the Ross Valley Fire Department has determined that adoption of the
proposed ordinance is exempt from the California Environmental Quality Act because it
has no potential for causing a significant effect on the environment (California Code of
Regulations, Title 14, § 15061(b)(3)).

SECTION 3-3.810. AMENDMENTS MADE TO THE 2025
CALIFORNIA FIRE CODE, 2024
INTERNATIONAL FIRE CODE
The 2025 California Fire Code and the adopted portions of the 2024 International
Fire Code, as applicable, are amended and changed in the following respects:
CHAPTER 1
SCOPE AND ADMINISTRATION
Section 101.1 of Chapter 1 is amended to read as follows:
Section 101.1 Title. These regulations and locally adopted standards
shall be known as the Fire Code of the Town of San Anselmo, hereinafter
referred to as “this code.”
Section 102.5 of Chapter 1 is hereby amended to read as follows:
Section 102.5 Application of residential code. Where structures are
designed and constructed in accordance with the California Residential
Code, the provisions of this code shall apply as follows:
1.
Construction and designed provisions: Provisions of this code
pertaining to the exterior of the structure shall apply including,
but not limited to, premises identification, fire apparatus access
and water supplies. Provisions of this code pertaining to the
interior of the structure when specifically required by this code
including, but not limited to, Section 903.2 and Chapter 12 shall
apply. Where interior or exterior systems or devices are installed,
construction permits required by Section 105.7 of this code shall
also apply
2.
Administrative, operational, and maintenance provisions: all
such provisions of this code shall apply.
Section 102.7.3 is hereby added to Chapter 1 and shall read as follows:
Section 102.7.3 Nationally Recognized Listed Products . Any
installation of products and equipment due to permits required by this
Code shall be Labeled and Listed, as defined in Section 202.
Section 104.1.1 is hereby added to Chapter 1 and shall read as follows:
Section 104.1.1 Supplemental Rules, Regulations and Standards or
Policies. The Fire Code Official is authorized to render interpretations of

and to develop Fire Protection Standards or Policies to carry out the

agencies shall have authority to render necessary assistance in plan
review, inspection, code interpretation, enforcement, investigation and

section 105.5, the following permits shall be obtained from the Fire Prevention
Bureau of the Ross Valley Fire Department prior to engaging in the following
activities, operations, practices or functions:
1. Fire Protection Plan. An operational permit is required to implement
a fire protection plan.
2. Radioactive material. An operational permit is required to store or
handle at any installation more than 1 micro curie (37,000 Becquerel) of
radioactive material not contained in a sealed source or more that 1
millicurie (37,000,000 Becquerel) of radioactive material in a sealed
source or sources, or any amount of radioactive material for which
specific license from the Nuclear Regulatory Commission is required.

105.6.26 Local Construction Permits. In addition to the permits required by
section 105.6, the following permits shall be obtained from the Ross Valley Fire

Section 113.4 of Chapter 1 is hereby amended to read as follows:
Section 113.4 Violation Penalties. Persons who shall violate a provision
of this code or shall fail to comply with any of the requirements thereof or
who shall erect, install, alter repair or do work in violation of the approved
construction documents or directive of the Fire Code Official, or of a permit
or certificate used under provisions of this code, shall be guilty of a
misdemeanor, punishable by a fine of not more than $500 dollars or by
imprisonment not exceeding 180 days, or both such fine and
imprisonment. Each day that a violation continues after due notice has
been served shall be deemed a separate offense.
The imposition of one penalty of any violation shall not excuse the violation
or permit it to continue; and all such persons shall be required to correct or
remedy such violations or defects within a reasonable time; and when not
otherwise specified, each day that a violation continues after due notice
has been served shall be deemed a separate offense.
Section 113.4.1.1 of Chapter 1 is added to read as follows:
Section 113.4.1.1 Abatement of clearance of brush or vegetative
growth from structures. The executive body is authorized to instruct the
Chief to give notice to the owner of the property upon which conditions
regulated by section 304.1.2 of Chapter 3 exists to correct such conditions.
If the owner fails to correct such conditions, the executive body is
authorized to cause the same to be done and make the expense of such
correction a lien upon the property where such condition exists.
Section 114.4 of Chapter 1 is hereby amended to read as follows:

100

=== page 101 ===
Section 114.4 Failure to Comply. Any person who shall continue any
work after having been served with a stop work order, except such work as that
person is directed to perform to remove a violation or unsafe condition, shall be
liable to a fine not less than $500 dollars or more than $1000 dollars.
Section 116 is hereby added Chapter 1 and shall read as follows:
Section 116 DAMAGES AND EXPENSE RECOVERY
Section 116.1 Damages and Expense Recovery. The expense of securing
any emergency that is within the responsibility for enforcement of the Fire
Chief as given in Section 104 is a charge against the person who caused
the emergency. Damages and expenses incurred by any public agency
having jurisdiction or any public agency assisting the agency having
jurisdiction shall constitute a debt of such person and shall be collectible
by the Fire Chief for proper distribution in the same manner as in the case
of an obligation under contract expressed or implied. Expenses as stated
above shall include, but not be limited to, equipment and personnel
committed and any payments required by the public agency to outside
business firms requested by the public agency to secure the emergency,
monitor remediation, and clean up .
CHAPTER 2
DEFINITIONS
Section 202 of Chapter 2 is hereby amended by adding the following general
definition:
ALL WEATHER SURFACE shall mean A/C paving, or concrete capable of
supporting 75,000 lb. gross vehicle weight. Grades up to and including
18% may be of A/C paving. Grades greater than 18% shall be of concrete
kurf-cut so as to allow for water run-off and traction.
Exception: Materials approved by the Fire Code Official.
COVERINGS shall mean materials including, but not limited to gypsum
board, paneling, floor boards, lathe and plaster, wood paneling, brick and
mortar, or other materials attached to rough framing of the building
elements. ‘Coverings’ do not include carpet, linoleum, tile, wall paper, or
other decorative finishes.
EXTERIOR WILDFIRE PROTECTION SYSTEM An approved system of
devices and equipment which is automatically or manually activated to
discharge water and or an approved fire-extinguishing agent onto the
8 Item 2.1
Attachment 1
101

=== page 102 ===
structure and or the exterior of the structure to hydrate the Immediate
Zone 0 (0-5 feet from the home, including the home) and the Intermediate
Zone 1 (5-30 feet from the home).
FIRE ROAD shall mean those improved or unimproved roads, public or
private, that provide access for firefighting equipment and personnel to
undeveloped areas.
HOME BACKUP GENERATOR. A permanent, fixed installation, internal
combustion engine-driven device that provides temporary electrical power
to a Group R-3 and R-4 Occupancies.
PRE-PLANS shall mean detailed plans of target hazard buildings. These
pre-plans include information on the building's location, occupancy,
hazards, fire department connections and hydrants, building layout, and
other pertinent data that would assist the fire department in case of an
emergency.
PUBLIC STORAGE FACILITY shall mean any business that sells, leases
or rents space to the public that is enclosed, whether it is a building,
storage container or similar configuration.
SPARK ARRESTOR shall mean a chimney device constructed in a
skillful-like manner. The net free area of a spark arrestor shall not be less
than four times the net free area of the outlet of the chimney. The spark
arrestor screen shall have heat and corrosion resistance equivalent to
12-gauge wire, 19-gauge galvanized wire or 24-gauge stainless steel.
Opening shall not permit the passage of spheres having a diameter larger
than 1/2 inch and shall not block the passage of spheres having a
diameter of less than 3/8 inch.
SUBSTANTIAL REMODEL shall mean the renovation of any structure,
which combined with any additions to the structure, affects a floor area
which exceeds fifty percent of the existing floor area of the structure
within any 36 month period. When any changes are made in the
building, such as walls, columns, beams or girders, floor or ceiling joists
and coverings, roof rafters, roof diaphragms, foundations, piles or
retaining walls or similar components, the floor area of all rooms affected
by such changes shall be included in computing floor areas for the
purpose s of applying this definition. This definition does not apply to the
replacement and upgrading of residential roof coverings, repair or
replacement of foundations, the installation of energy efficient windows
of like size, or the removal of interior wall coverings solely for the
installation of insulation.
9 Item 2.1
Attachment 1
102

=== page 103 ===
TARGET HAZARD is defined as a location or plausible scenario in which
a fire department or fire district could quickly become overwhelmed and for
which additional resources, now scarce, would be needed.
TEMPORARY shall mean any use for a period of less than 90 days, where
not otherwise referenced.
UNWARRANTED ALARM shall mean the giving, signaling or transition of
an alarm notification to a public fire station or emergency communication
center when such alarm is the result of a defective condition of an alarm
system, system servicing testing, construction activities, ordinary
household activities, false alarm or other cause when no such danger
exists.

Agricultural crop production including cultivation, drying, processing and

Section 324.3 Fire Apparatus Access. All public storage facilities
shall have fire apparatus access roads provided in accordance with

Section 324.4 Storage of Flammable and Combustible
Liquids and Hazardous Materials. The storage of hazardous materials
or flammable or combustible liquids in public storage facilities is
prohibited. Such facilities shall post legible and durable sign(s) to indicate
the same in a manner and location(s) as specified by the Fire Code Official.
This section shall apply to new and existing public storage facilities.
